    "originalFile": "% Runtime overhead (inline paragraph, no subsection header to save space)\n\n\\paragraph{Runtime overhead.}\n\\label{sec:runtime}\nS1 overhead is $+$13.1\\% on SciERC, lower on DocRED due to amortization over longer sequences; under LoRA, $+$10.0\\% with 37\\% lower absolute latency.\nConstraint compilation is one-time $<$0.5\\,s; scaling benchmarks confirm constant overhead (2--3\\%) up to 200 entity types and 1{,}000 relations (Appendix~\\ref{app:scalability}).\n\n\\paragraph{Beam search compatibility.}\n\\label{sec:beam-results}\nWith LoRA, beam search ($k{=}4$)~\\cite{anderson2017guided} eliminates all four violation types on SciERC (R-F1 44--46); zero-shot beam achieves DR${=}$0\\% on both datasets (CF response is model-dependent; Appendix~\\ref{app:beam-search}).\nTotal latency: 1.54$\\times$ (beam 1.32$\\times$ $+$ constraint 1.17$\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:beam-latency}).\n\n% exp_ids: r16_70b_scierc_zs_s1, r16_70b_docred_zs_s1, 70b_lora_scierc, 70b_lora_docred_200\n\\paragraph{70B evaluation.}\n\\label{sec:70b-results}\nOn Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct (AWQ INT4), zero-shot S1 eliminates DR on both datasets: SciERC 6.41\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0\\% (R-F1 11.24), DocRED 14.98\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0\\% (R-F1 13.05); CF rises to 3.50--7.01\\%, comparable to 8B zero-shot CF.\n% exp_ids: eval_70b_lora_scierc_s1_constrained, eval_70b_lora_docred_200_s1_constrained\nLoRA fine-tuning confirms scale-independent composability: SciERC DR 1.50\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0\\% (R-F1 44.53, $-$0.20\\,pp); DocRED (200-document subset; Appendix~\\ref{app:power-analysis}) DR 0.22\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0\\% (R-F1 43.95, $+$0.15\\,pp); CF drops from 3.50\\% (zero-shot) to 0.18--1.33\\% (LoRA), consistent with the 8B pattern (9.90\\%\\,$\\to$\\,1.18\\%; \\S\\ref{sec:composability}).\n\n% exp_ids: trie_qwen_ft_scierc, trie_qwen_zs_scierc, trie_llama_ft_scierc, trie_llama_zs_scierc\n\\paragraph{Comparison with grammar-constrained approaches.}\n\\label{sec:constrained-baselines}\nOur grammar-only ablation (Table~\\ref{tab:scierc-ablation}, row~2) implements the same token-level constraints as GCD~\\cite{geng2023gcd} and BoostCD~\\cite{boostcd2025}---CFG-based JSON structure enforcement plus vocabulary membership masking---providing a direct head-to-head comparison: RV/ET$\\to$0\\% but only 3\\% DR reduction, vs.\\ S1's 94\\%, confirming that CFG enforcement alone is insufficient for type-valid IE.\nWithout JSON structural enforcement, trie-only vocabulary constraints yield R-F1 19.95--41.16 on SciERC LoRA (vs.\\ OntoCoDec's 43.77--45.47) with 9.1--50.8\\% parse failure (Appendix~\\ref{app:trie-baseline});   "content": "[Director 指令] **Error Analysis 填充 + 主文 claim**\n\n数据已就绪：`artifacts/_project/paper/error_analysis_cases.json`。两个任务：\n\n### 1. 主文加 quantitative claim\n\n在 section5_scierc_draft.tex 的 FT composability 段或 §5.4 runtime 段加一句：\n\"Error analysis confirms that constraint enforcement has minimal impact on recall: of 514 false negatives under LoRA + S1 on SciERC, only one (0.2\\%) was blocked by constraint enforcement; the remaining 99.8\\% reflect model limitations unrelated to constraints (relation type confusion, missing entities, zero-relation outputs; Appendix~\\ref{app:error-analysis}).\"\n\n### 2. Appendix error analysis 填充\n\n在 appendix_case_studies.tex 的预留框架中填入 6 个代表性案例（每类 2 个）：\n\n**False Negatives（2 个）**：\n- fn_001（唯一的 constraint-blocked FN）：Evaluate-for 被 CF 阻断，unconstrained 可生成\n- fn_004（典型 relation type confusion）：Part-of→Used-for 混淆，约束无法纠正\n\n**False Positives（2 个）**：\n- fp_001（schema-valid 但错误）：Used-for 代替 Part-of，schema 允许两者\n- fp_007（hallucinated relation）：忽略否定语义 \"show little gain from\"\n\n**CF Violations（2 个）**：\n- cf_001（Generic↔Method 多义）：'approach' 在不同关系中被分配不同类型\n- cf_005（Material↔Task 复合名词）：'person re-identification datasets' 含任务+数据双重语义\n\n每个案例格式：上下文引用 + gold vs predicted + 1-2 句分析。保持简洁，整个 appendix subsection 控制在半页到 2/3 页。\n\n最后加一段 summary：\n\"The error analysis reveals a clear division of labor: structural constraints eliminate type-invalid outputs (DR→0.09%), but semantic ambiguity within schema-valid space (relation type confusion, entity type polysemy) remains the dominant error source. Only 1 of 514 false negatives (0.2%) was attributable to constraint enforcement, confirming that the recall cost of type constraints is negligible.\"\n\n完成后编译检查。"
}

"Error analysis confirms that constraint enforcement has minimal impact on recall: of 514 false negatives under LoRA + S1 on SciERC, only one (0.2\%) was blocked by constraint enforcement; the remaining 99.8\% reflect model limitations unrelated to constraints (relation type confusion, missing entities, zero-relation outputs; Appendix~\ref{app:error-analysis})."

### 2. Appendix error analysis 填充

在 appendix_case_studies.tex 的预留框架中填入 6 个代表性案例（每类 2 个）：

**False Negatives（2 个）**：
- fn_001（唯一的 constraint-blocked FN）：Evaluate-for 被 CF 阻断，unconstrained 可生成
- fn_004（典型 relation type confusion）：Part-of→Used-for 混淆，约束无法纠正

**False Positives（2 个）**：
- fp_001（schema-valid 但错误）：Used-for 代替 Part-of，schema 允许两者
- fp_007（hallucinated relation）：忽略否定语义 "show little gain from"

**CF Violations（2 个）**：
- cf_001（Generic↔Method 多义）：'approach' 在不同关系中被分配不同类型
- cf_005（Material↔Task 复合名词）：'person re-identification datasets' 含任务+数据双重语义

每个案例格式：上下文引用 + gold vs predicted + 1-2 句分析。保持简洁，整个 appendix subsection 控制在半页到 2/3 页。

最后加一段 summary：
"The error analysis reveals a clear division of labor: structural constraints eliminate type-invalid outputs (DR→0.09%), but semantic ambiguity within schema-valid space (relation type confusion, entity type polysemy) remains the dominant error source. Only 1 of 514 false negatives (0.2%) was attributable to constraint enforcement, confirming that the recall cost of type constraints is negligible."

完成后编译检查。

10	Table~\ref{tab:scierc-ablation} presents the five-way ablation on SciERC (Qwen3-8B, zero-shot, 551 documents); only the active constraint layers differ across rows.
11

67	
68	% ──────────────────────────────────────────
69	%  (a) Core result
70	% ──────────────────────────────────────────
71	\paragraph{Zero-shot diagnostic.}
72	% phase2_scierc_unconstrained: DR=0.1537; phase2_scierc_full_s1: DR=0.0093
73	% phase2_scierc_unconstrained: R-F1=0.0823; phase2_scierc_full_s1: R-F1=0.0836
74	% bootstrap: delta=+0.13pp, CI=[-0.24,+0.58], p=0.5824
75	S1 reduces DR from 15.37\% to 0.93\% ($\downarrow$94\%) while R-F1 is unchanged (8.23\,$\to$\,8.36, $p{=}0.58$).
76	Per-relation DR drops to $\leq$2.82\%, reaching 0\% on 4/7 relations (Appendix~\ref{app:per-relation-landscape}).
77	Post-hoc filtering discards up to 48\% of predictions rather than correcting them (Appendix~\ref{app:posthoc-extended}).
78	Total type-violation burden (Tot.\,=\,DR+CF) decreases from 15.45\% to 10.83\% ($\downarrow$30\%), reflecting \emph{violation conversion}: DR enforcement replaces ontologically invalid triples with individually valid but type-inconsistent ones (CF 0.08\%\,$\to$\,9.90\%); under fine-tuning, Tot.\ drops to 1.18\% with CF\,$\leq$\,1.4\% (Table~\ref{tab:composability}).
79	
80	% ──────────────────────────────────────────
81	%  (b) Grammar layer
82	% ──────────────────────────────────────────
83	\paragraph{Layer contributions.}
84	% phase2_scierc_grammar_only: RV=0, ET=0, DR=0.1484, R-F1=0.0823
85	Grammar-only eliminates RV and ET with no F1 impact while DR remains at 14.84\%, the ceiling of grammar-based decoding~\cite{geng2023gcd}; grammar-only CF is near-zero (0.08\%), so CF originates from the semantic layer.
86	Semantic-only reduces DR by only 24\% and cannot eliminate RV or ET; this is the \emph{empirical enabling dependency}: adding the grammar layer yields a further 92\% reduction (11.65\%\,$\to$\,0.93\%).
87	
88	
89	% ──────────────────────────────────────────
90	%  (d) S1 vs S2
91	% ──────────────────────────────────────────
92	\paragraph{S1 vs.\ S2.}
93	S2 achieves comparable DR (0.97\%) but lower R-F1 and CF; we adopt S1.
94	% phase2_scierc_full_s2: DR=0.0097, R-F1=0.0803, CF=0.0803, triples=1644
95	
96	% ──────────────────────────────────────────
97	%  (e) Composability with Fine-Tuning
98	%  exp_ids: w5_lora_scierc_unconstrained_eval, w5_lora_scierc_constrained_eval,
99	%           w5_llama_lora_scierc_unconstrained_eval, w5_llama_lora_scierc_constrained_eval,
100	%           w5_qwen_docred_lora_unconstrained_eval, w5_qwen_docred_lora_constrained_eval,
101	%           w5_llama_docred_lora_unconstrained_eval, w5_llama_docred_lora_constrained_eval
102	% ──────────────────────────────────────────
103	\paragraph{Composability with fine-tuning.}
104	\label{sec:composability}
105	% exp_ids: bootstrap_ci_ft_composability.json
106	Fine-tuning alone reduces DR to 1--2\% but leaves residual violations (Table~\ref{tab:composability}); adding S1 yields DR\,$\leq$\,0.09\% with no significant $\Delta$R-F1 after Bonferroni correction (min.\ $p{=}0.014$; 95\% CIs $\subseteq [-0.76, {+}0.22]$\,pp; removed triples are 97.6--100\% false positives).
107	CF drops from $+$9.82\,pp (zero-shot) to $+$1.18\,pp (fine-tuned; Appendix~\ref{app:cf-distribution}); beam ($k{=}4$) with LoRA eliminates all four violation types on SciERC (R-F1 43.60, 46.18; \S\ref{sec:beam-results}).
108

146	% ──────────────────────────────────────────
147	%  (f) Downstream impact of DR elimination
148	%  exp_ids: task_e_lucky_violation_analysis
149	% ──────────────────────────────────────────
150	\paragraph{Downstream impact.}
151	\label{sec:downstream-impact}
152	Among constraint-removed triples, only 0.49\% match gold labels on average (``lucky violations''), confirming $<$0.5\% recall cost; on Llama SciERC all five are recovered via legal type paths (zero churn; Appendix~\ref{app:lucky-violations}).
153

grammar-only CF is near-zero (0.08\\%), so CF originates from the semantic layer.\nSemantic-only reduces DR by only 24\\% and cannot eliminate RV or ET; this is the \\emph{empirical enabling dependency}: adding the grammar layer yields a further 92\\% reduction (11.65\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0.93\\%).\n\n\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n%  (d) S1 vs S2\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n\\paragraph{S1 vs.\\ S2.}\nS2 achieves comparable DR (0.97\\%) but lower R-F1 and CF; we adopt S1.\n% phase2_scierc_full_s2: DR=0.0097, R-F1=0.0803, CF=0.0803, triples=1644\n\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n%  (e) Composability with Fine-Tuning\n%  exp_ids: w5_lora_scierc_unconstrained_eval, w5_lora_scierc_constrained_eval,\n%           w5_llama_lora_scierc_unconstrained_eval, w5_llama_lora_scierc_constrained_eval,\n%           w5_qwen_docred_lora_unconstrained_eval, w5_qwen_docred_lora_constrained_eval,\n%           w5_llama_docred_lora_unconstrained_eval, w5_llama_docred_lora_constrained_eval\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n\\paragraph{Composability with fine-tuning.}\n\\label{sec:composability}\n% exp_ids: bootstrap_ci_ft_composability.json\nFine-tuning alone reduces DR to 1--2\\% but leaves residual violations (Table~\\ref{tab:composability}); {
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_6/projects/schema_semantic_constrained_ie/artifacts/_project/paper/appendix_case_studies.tex",
  "old_string": "\\subsection{Error Analysis: Constraint Impact on Prediction Quality}\n\\label{app:error-analysis}\n\n% Content will be populated from error_analysis_cases.json\n% Categories: (1) False negatives from constraint blocking\n%             (2) False positives passing constraints\n%             (3) CF violations from fallback",
  "new_string": "\\subsection{Error Analysis: Constraint Impact on Prediction Quality}\n\\label{app:error-analysis}\n\n% exp_id: w5_lora_scierc_full_s1_eval (514 FN, 668 FP, 21 CF)\nWe analyze errors from Qwen3-8B LoRA + S1 on SciERC (551 documents; R-F1 43.77, 460 TP, 668 FP, 514 FN) across three categories.\n\n\\paragraph{False negatives: constraint-blocked vs.\\ model-limited.}\nOf 514 false negatives, only one (0.2\\%) is attributable to constraint enforcement:\n\n\\noindent\\textit{fn\\_001} (constraint-blocked): ``\\textit{Experiments on the TREC Blog track test set show that both groups of credibility indicators significantly improve retrieval effectiveness.}'' \\\\\nGold: Evaluate-for(retrieval effectiveness\\textsuperscript{Metric}, credibility indicators\\textsuperscript{OtherSci}).\nThe unconstrained model generates this triple correctly, but under S1, conflicting type assignments to `credibility indicators' (OtherScientificTerm in entity list vs.\\ Method in Evaluate-for's tail) trigger a CF violation, redirecting generation to a reversed Used-for triple.\n\n\\noindent\\textit{fn\\_004} (relation type confusion): ``\\textit{Amorph recognizes NE items in two stages: dictionary lookup and rule application.}'' \\\\\nGold: Part-of(dictionary lookup\\textsuperscript{Meth}, Amorph\\textsuperscript{Meth}).\nThe model predicts Used-for instead of Part-of---both are schema-valid for Method$\\to$Method pairs, so constraints provide no corrective signal. This Part-of$\\leftrightarrow$Used-for confusion is the dominant FN pattern.\n\n\\paragraph{False positives: schema-valid but incorrect.}\n\n\\noindent\\textit{fp\\_001} (wrong relation type): Same sentence as fn\\_004. The model generates Used-for(dictionary lookup, Amorph) where gold is Part-of. Used-for is the majority relation in SciERC (56\\% of all relations), causing systematic over-prediction. Since both relation types are schema-valid for the entity type pair, constraints cannot distinguish them.\n\n\\noindent\\textit{fp\\_007} (hallucinated relation): ``\\textit{The classifiers show little gain from information about meeting context.}'' \\\\\nNo gold relation exists. The model generates Used-for(meeting context\\textsuperscript{OtherSci}, classifiers\\textsuperscript{Meth}), ignoring the negation in ``show little gain from.'' The instrumental preposition ``from'' triggers relation generation despite the negative result.\n\n\\paragraph{CF violations: entity type polysemy.}\n\n\\noindent\\textit{cf\\_001} (Generic$\\leftrightarrow$Method): ``\\textit{A basic reason behind this is that the overall approach extends the approach described in a companion paper\\ldots}'' \\\\\nThe entity `approach' receives Generic in one Used-for relation and Method in another. The gold type is Generic; the polysemy of `approach' (generic reference vs.\\ specific method) causes per-relation type assignment to diverge.\n\n\\noindent\\textit{cf\\_005} (Material$\\leftrightarrow$Task): ``\\textit{Experimental evaluations on two challenging person re-identification datasets demonstrate the superiority of the proposed model\\ldots}'' \\\\\nThe compound noun `person re-identification datasets' contains both a task component and a data component. The model assigns Material in one Evaluate-for relation and Task in another within the same sentence.\n\n\\paragraph{Summary.}\nStructural constraints eliminate type-invalid outputs (DR$\\to$0.09\\%), but semantic ambiguity within schema-valid space---relation type confusion, entity type polysemy---remains the dominant error source. Only 1 of 514 false negatives (0.2\\%) was attributable to constraint enforcement, confirming that the recall cost of type constraints is negligible."
}

    "newString": "\\subsection{Error Analysis: Constraint Impact on Prediction Quality}\n\\label{app:error-analysis}\n\n% exp_id: w5_lora_scierc_full_s1_eval (514 FN, 668 FP, 21 CF)\nWe analyze errors from Qwen3-8B LoRA + S1 on SciERC (551 documents; R-F1 43.77, 460 TP, 668 FP, 514 FN) across three categories.\n\n\\paragraph{False negatives: constraint-blocked vs.\\ model-limited.}\nOf 514 false negatives, only one (0.2\\%) is attributable to constraint enforcement:\n\n\\noindent\\textit{fn\\_001} (constraint-blocked): ``\\textit{Experiments on the TREC Blog track test set show that both groups of credibility indicators significantly improve retrieval effectiveness.}'' \\\\\nGold: Evaluate-for(retrieval effectiveness\\textsuperscript{Metric}, credibility indicators\\textsuperscript{OtherSci}).\nThe unconstrained model generates this triple correctly, but under S1, conflicting type assignments to `credibility indicators' (OtherScientificTerm in entity list vs.\\ Method in Evaluate-for's tail) trigger a CF violation, redirecting generation to a reversed Used-for triple.\n\n\\noindent\\textit{fn\\_004} (relation type confusion): ``\\textit{Amorph recognizes NE items in two stages: dictionary lookup and rule application.}'' \\\\\nGold: Part-of(dictionary lookup\\textsuperscript{Meth}, Amorph\\textsuperscript{Meth}).\nThe model predicts Used-for instead of Part-of---both are schema-valid for Method$\\to$Method pairs, so constraints provide no corrective signal. grammar-only CF is near-zero (0.08\\%), so CF originates from the semantic layer.\nSemantic-only reduces DR by only 24\\% and cannot eliminate RV or ET; this is the \\emph{empirical enabling dependency}: adding the grammar layer yields a further 92\\% reduction (11.65\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0.93\\%).\n\n\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n%  (d) S1 vs S2\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n\\paragraph{S1 vs.\\ S2.}\nS2 achieves comparable DR (0.97\\%) but lower R-F1 and CF; we adopt S1.\n% phase2_scierc_full_s2: DR=0.0097, R-F1=0.0803, CF=0.0803, triples=1644\n\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n%  (e) Composability with Fine-Tuning\n%  exp_ids: w5_lora_scierc_unconstrained_eval, w5_lora_scierc_constrained_eval,\n%           w5_llama_lora_scierc_unconstrained_eval, w5_llama_lora_scierc_constrained_eval,\n%           w5_qwen_docred_lora_unconstrained_eval, w5_qwen_docred_lora_constrained_eval,\n%           w5_llama_docred_lora_unconstrained_eval, w5_llama_docred_lora_constrained_eval\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n\\paragraph{Composability with fine-tuning.}\n\\label{sec:composability}\n% exp_ids: bootstrap_ci_ft_composability.json\nFine-tuning alone reduces DR to 1--2\\% but leaves residual violations (Table~\\ref{tab:composability});     "originalFile": "% Runtime overhead (inline paragraph, no subsection header to save space)\n\n\\paragraph{Runtime overhead.}\n\\label{sec:runtime}\nS1 overhead is $+$13.1\\% on SciERC, lower on DocRED due to amortization over longer sequences; under LoRA, $+$10.0\\% with 37\\% lower absolute latency.\nConstraint compilation is one-time $<$0.5\\,s; scaling benchmarks confirm constant overhead (2--3\\%) up to 200 entity types and 1{,}000 relations (Appendix~\\ref{app:scalability}).\n\n\\paragraph{Beam search compatibility.}\n\\label{sec:beam-results}\nWith LoRA, beam search ($k{=}4$)~\\cite{anderson2017guided} eliminates all four violation types on SciERC (R-F1 44--46); zero-shot beam achieves DR${=}$0\\% on both datasets (CF response is model-dependent; Appendix~\\ref{app:beam-search}).\nTotal latency: 1.54$\\times$ (beam 1.32$\\times$ $+$ constraint 1.17$\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:beam-latency}).\n\n% exp_ids: r16_70b_scierc_zs_s1, r16_70b_docred_zs_s1, 70b_lora_scierc, 70b_lora_docred_200\n\\paragraph{70B evaluation.}\n\\label{sec:70b-results}\nOn Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct (AWQ INT4), zero-shot S1 eliminates DR on both datasets: SciERC 6.41\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0\\% (R-F1 11.24), DocRED 14.98\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0\\% (R-F1 13.05); CF rises to 3.50--7.01\\%, comparable to 8B zero-shot CF.\n% exp_ids: eval_70b_lora_scierc_s1_constrained, eval_70b_lora_docred_200_s1_constrained\nLoRA fine-tuning confirms scale-independent composability: SciERC DR 1.50\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0\\% (R-F1 44.53, $-$0.20\\,pp); \\S\\ref{sec:composability}).\n\n% exp_ids: trie_qwen_ft_scierc, trie_qwen_zs_scierc, trie_llama_ft_scierc, trie_llama_zs_scierc\n\\paragraph{Comparison with grammar-constrained approaches.}\n\\label{sec:constrained-baselines}\nOur grammar-only ablation (Table~\\ref{tab:scierc-ablation}, row~2) implements the same token-level constraints as GCD~\\cite{geng2023gcd} and BoostCD~\\cite{boostcd2025}---CFG-based JSON structure enforcement plus vocabulary membership masking---providing a direct head-to-head comparison: RV/ET$\\to$0\\% but only 3\\% DR reduction, vs.\\ S1's 94\\%, confirming that CFG enforcement alone is insufficient for type-valid IE (see Appendix~\\ref{app:gcd-comparison} for a detailed comparison).\nWithout JSON structural enforcement, trie-only vocabulary constraints yield R-F1 19.95--41.16 on SciERC LoRA (vs.\\ OntoCoDec's 43.77--45.47) with 9.1--50.8\\% parse failure (Appendix~\\ref{app:trie-baseline});
